Every status below is the contract audit's (136 obligations, first done
2026-10-05, refreshed 2026-10-28, with the 28-29 Oct work applied), then
re-checked: on 29 Oct four independent reviewers held each of the 64 items
then marked DONE against the grant's own wording, and 27 of them did not
meet it (2 more were unclear). Those are re-stated as PARTIAL or DRIFT, each
with what the review found; three of them (WP2-04, WP2-07, WP4-20) were
fixed the same night. On 2 Nov the WP2 and WP4 correction notes were
confirmed sent (WP2-12, WP4-21), the Gazebo rail first flew end-to-end
(ARCH-16), and the paraphraser became the separate service the
grant names (WP2-13), and the audit record took the grant's field meanings
(WP3-11). On 3 Nov a GCS-set GeoFence (WP3-10) and per-topic SROS 2
permissions (X-15) were flown, the copies of the rule clock and the rule
verdicts were folded into the Shield's evaluator (WP3-22 and X-13, DRIFT to
PARTIAL), the Shield node's buffer and index were conformed (WP3-23), and
every shipped policy became WGS84 with a lazy local view, distance_envelope
was enforced and MSL given a ground source (WP1-14: DRIFT to PARTIAL; WP1-07,
X-11 still PARTIAL, with less open). On 6 Nov `scope` became a required
field (IR schema 4.0.0), the last of the four ConstraintBase fields the
grant requires, and every shipped rule states it (WP1-01: PARTIAL to DONE).
On 7 Nov OpenVLA-7B flew with the CSP in its prompt, the A/B on three seeds
and eight paraphrases issued by the paraphraser service (AirSimNH, not
KPI-grade): WP2-01, WP2-05, WP2-09, WP2-18, WP4-09 and WP4-10 to DONE.
On 13 Nov the KPI-grade nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN2 (116214f, 580
KPI-grade episodes) was scored, and every row it touches was held again
against the grant pages' own words (the review note at the end): the P0
escape rate (WP3-14), the tracked KPIs - mean repair magnitude (WP3-16),
mean time to safe (WP3-17), repair success (WP3-20, X-02), repair count
(X-03), mission success (WP4-08) - and the six-field manifest (WP4-12) to
DONE; fail-safe trigger correctness (WP3-15), the tick budget (WP3-13), the
written KPI report (WP4-17) and the rows that need a push, a release or
another rail stay PARTIAL, each with its gap. On 14 Nov WP3-13 moved to
DONE (no tick over 100 ms end to end at the fixed node code: ge1113, 0 of
95,160, unloaded; hs09 and hs10 loaded); WP3-07 stays PARTIAL although hs10
at 8a473fa flew G2, G4, G6, X2 and X3, the edges no rail had taken: SET_MODE
LOITER is driven on no hil flight (the GUIDED hold, lab decision 7, PI confirmation pending); WP4-17's report is
committed from a clean tree (docs/data/kpi_ntFIN2), its delivery remains
(CHANGELOG 2026-11-14 (hs10 ...)).
On 17 Nov the project decided that the dated deliverables are reported on
what was delivered and where it is. WP1-21 (Policy DSL + IR), WP3-01 (the ROS 2 Shield node), ARCH-16
(the Gazebo rail), ARCH-17 (the mid-term delivery) and ARCH-23 (the
paraphraser) moved to DONE; ARCH-24 moved to DONE with the team stated as
in the grant. ARCH-15 stays DRIFT: G1's four-condition test passed on two
sets of three runs over ArduPilot's SIM_AirSim JSON interface, not the
HIL_GPS / HIL_SENSOR messages the gate names, and the gate's swap rule was
not applied (section 7).
On 23 Nov an independent recount of the open rows, with an adversarial
check of every proposed status change, moved WP3-22 to DONE (FenceGuard
judges with the Shield's ruler and clearance_breached, "grid" refused,
flown 19-20 Nov) and WP3-13 back to PARTIAL (the nightly ntFIN4 at 59e19a5
flew 1 of 183,909 ticks at 105.5 ms, a hot-apply tick on a code path older
than 71aabb3, the fix not committed); the proposed moves of WP3-04,
WP3-07, WP3-19, X-11 and X-13 to DONE were refuted, and 29 rows got
refreshed evidence and gaps. On 25 Nov X-11 moved to DONE after the hil
zone-ridge cells flew with the terrain feed (x11r1125) and an independent
review of the row against the grant's wording (three reviewers, all DONE).
On 26 Nov WP3-04 moved to DONE after an independent review (three
reviewers, all DONE): the stack-level rotation the 25 Nov review named
is gone (35df373, release 0.7.1 a3d616e, flown
smREL071); the same review kept ARCH-08, WP4-11 and WP4-19 PARTIAL.
On 26 Nov WP1-14 moved to DONE after an independent review of the third WGS84-only fix (2 of 3; the dissent, legacy hash forms matched across eras, was closed by a fourth fix).
On 27 Nov the policy bundles were signed under the Lab CA (a lab decision

**114 DONE, 21 PARTIAL, 1 DRIFT, 0 MISSING.** DONE means the code
or the artefact the grant names exists and matches the grant's words; for a
KPI row it means a KPI-grade value meets them; for any other row it never
means KPI-grade.

## 1. Summary (to write last)

Points the summary will carry, each already backed by section 4:

- The Guardrail exists in the grant's structure: a Policy DSL with a typed
  IR and signed bundles (WP1), a Prefix Compiler that emits a Constraint
  Summary Pack (WP2), a Safety Shield with an escalation FSM in front of
  ArduPilot (WP3), and a Stress Testing harness with a scenario library,
  episode bundles and a KPI report generator (WP4). One obligation is
  built differently from the grant's words (DRIFT: ARCH-15, Appendix A;
  the deviations in section 7) and the report says so.
- No P0 violation has escaped the Shield on any rail flown. KPI-grade, the
  figure of record: 0 escapes on 17,078 P0-violation ticks over the 650
  Shield-on missions of the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN4 (59e19a5,
  22-23 Nov), against 0.6500 for its Shield-off control; ntFIN3 (71aabb3,
  21 Nov): 0 on 17,457 over 591, control 0.6458. Before it: 0
  escapes on 16,703 P0-violation ticks over the 577 Shield-on missions of
  the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN2, against 0.6375 for its Shield-off
  control (section 2); with the supplement ntFIN2s, 0 on 16,760 ticks over
  586 Shield-on flights. The count is the Shield's log, as the grant
  defines it; ArduPilot carried the aircraft into a P0 zone in 21 of the
  ntFIN2 missions and in 3 of the supplement's (ntFIN2s_013-015, sweep.json
  `_true_state`) (WP3-14).
- A learned model (AerialVLA) has flown ArduPilot SITL behind the Shield and
  the FSM (29 Oct); the perception rail has flown over MAVROS 2 in the
  grant's `dev` topology (29 Oct).
- A text-reading VLA (OpenVLA-7B) has flown with the CSP in its prompt
  (7 Nov, AirSimNH, not KPI-grade); on base OpenVLA the CSP's effect on
  repairs is no larger than a rule-free prefix's (WP2-01, WP2-09).
- What the report cannot claim yet: fail-safe trigger correctness >= 99 %
  without a qualifier (on the figure of record ntFIN4, 648/648 = 1.000,
  Wilson 95 % low 0.994, on the adopted reading (r2), settled by the lab
  with the GeoFence backstop rule of 15 Nov and not yet confirmed by the
  PI; 630/630 without that rule; (r) 0.981, (a) 0.884; ntFIN3 before it
  587/589 with a lower bound of 0.988, WP3-15), the monitor tick within
  100 ms on every tick at the delivered revision (WP3-13: one ntFIN4 tick
  at 105.5 ms), a learned VLA in the KPI campaigns (they fly a stub pilot,
  ARCH-20), the `flight` topology (ARCH-14).

## 2. Scope and acceptance criteria

The locked KPIs and where each stands. "KPI-grade" means `is_kpi_grade`
accepts the run: `hil` or `flight` topology, sim_speedup 1.0, a clean
code_revision; tools/kpi_report.py also requires the signed replay bundle bound to the run and the campaign's acceptance.

**The figure of record since 23 Nov: the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN4**
at 59e19a5 (clean; 71aabb3 plus the eleven changes of CHANGELOG
2026-11-22 (code)), flown 22-23 Nov in 78,879.2 s on the same rig: 744
episodes, 90 refused before take-off (the 60 GPS-noise / latency missions
now fly on the rail), 654 missions flown, 653 KPI-grade (650 Shield on, 3
controls; one dyn-nfz-translate run refused by the setpoint-delivery
rule). P0 escape rate 0 on 17,078 P0-violation ticks (control 0.6500);
fail-safe (r2) 648/648 = 1.000, Wilson 95 % low 0.994 (meets 99 % at the
point and at 95 % confidence; 630/630 without the backstop rule);
`tick_ms` p99 28.4 ms, max 105.50 ms: 1 of 183,909 ticks over 100 ms, the
hot-apply tick of ntFIN4_236, whose generation write and CSP compile ran
before the setpoint (the fix followed the flight, not committed); mission
success 287/650, repair success 98.1 %, mean time to safe 3.39 s, 50.2
repairs per Shield-on mission; single_sha 653/653; replay at the flown
revision 355 identical, 298 last float digits, 0 not replayable, 1
pass-count difference (docs/data/kpi_ntFIN4/kpi_report_ntFIN4_2026-11-23.md,
replay_at_revision_ntFIN4.json; docs/FINAL-REPORT.md sections 2 and 5).
The smoke smFIN2 at the same revision flew all five rows of the Shield
test matrix (52 KPI-grade, P0 escape 0, (r2) 51/51; docs/data/kpi_smFIN2/).

**The figure of record 21-23 Nov: the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN3**
at 71aabb3 (clean), flown 21 Nov in 72,478.6 s on the same rig: 744
episodes, 150 refused before take-off, 594 missions flown, all KPI-grade
(591 Shield on, 3 controls). P0 escape rate 0 on 17,457 P0-violation ticks
(control 0.6458); fail-safe (r2) 587/589 = 0.997, Wilson 95 % low 0.988
(meets 99 % as the point estimate; the lower bound does not clear 0.99);
`tick_ms` max 74.52 ms and `e2e_ms` max 74.62 ms, 0 of 174,186 ticks over
100 ms; mission success 252/591, repair success 98.1 %, mean time to safe
3.38 s, 54.4 repairs per Shield-on mission
(docs/data/kpi_ntFIN3/kpi_report_ntFIN3_2026-11-21.md; docs/FINAL-REPORT.md
sections 2 and 5). The table below keeps the earlier campaign's figures.

**The earlier KPI-grade source: the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN2.** Profile
nightly at 116214f (clean), flown 12-13 Nov in 69,706 s on the AGX Orin
rig (ArduCopter 4.5.7 SITL and mavlink-router on the desktop; MAVROS 2,
the Shield and the stub pilots of guardrail/vla_stub.py on the Orin). Of
the profile's 744 episodes (225 cells), 153 were refused by the rail before
flight (69 paraphrase / prefix arms the stub cannot read, 69 stressors SITL
cannot realise - GPS noise, gusts - and 15 others, among them an RTL that
fails and a 30 m start at the GeoFence's ceiling), 591 missions were planned and
583 run folders written: 8 wind-beyond-authority missions never flew (the
desktop ran out of memory at the end) and 3 gps-dropout flights were not
accepted (the GPS denial was not applied). That leaves 580 KPI-grade
episodes, 577 Shield on and 3 Shield-off controls; all 583 manifests carry
the six fields with code_revision 116214f3e6ee, sim_speedup 1.0, topology
hil (demo/out/hil_ntFIN2/sweep.json; runs demo/out/hil_smoke/ntFIN2_*).
Scored on 13 Nov by `python tools/kpi_report.py --campaign
demo/out/hil_ntFIN2 --rescore-rail <its experiments/sweep_scenarios.py
--rescore-rail JSON>` (the report is not committed yet, WP4-17). The
supplement ntFIN2s (13 Nov, the same commit and profile) flew the 5

demo/out/hil_ntFIN2s`, two re-score tables; CHANGELOG 2026-11-13 (the

episodes: 589 KPI-grade, 586 Shield on. gps-dropout lane 15, window 6-13 s,
seeds 1002 and 1003 have no accepted flight in either campaign (the
denial's end applied 0.29-0.60 s late). A cell's three seeds are three flights of one deterministic scenario: no
flown cell consumes its seed (sweep.json `_seeds`), and the 580 episodes
have 389 distinct KPI signatures. Earlier KPI-grade runs: hil_smoke_03
(6 Nov), the smoke campaigns rh03, sm1110 and sm1111, the example sweep
ge1110 and the first nightly nt1111 (22d434b, 11-12 Nov).

## 3. System architecture and topologies

- 3.1 The action contract: 4-D body-frame action (vx, vy, vz, yaw_rate) at
  10 Hz; body-to-NED conversion in one place (sitl/mavlink_adapter_node.py).
  DONE: ARCH-05, WP3-03. Not as the grant words it: no VLA has emitted the
  10 Hz setpoint into ArduPilot (ARCH-01). Since 4 Nov the Shield takes
  and returns the body frame at its own boundary (`Shield.filter_body`) and
  body to local NED happens after the projection, in
  sitl/mavlink_adapter_node.py on the ROS 2 rail; every flying rail hands
  it the body action (ARCH-04: DRIFT to PARTIAL 4 Nov, CHANGELOG
  2026-11-04 (body frame); DONE 20 Nov). Since 783e626 (21 Nov, flown in
  smFIN2 and ntFIN4) the registry repair operators act in the body frame at
  the operator interface, since bec7135 (23 Nov, release 0.7.0) the last
  resorts too, and since 35df373 (26 Nov, release 0.7.1, flown in smREL071)
  no rotation wraps the stack: it carries the body action and reads
  North/East/up only through the tick's repair.BodyFrame (WP3-04: DONE
  26 Nov, independent review, 3 of 3). The simulator demo loops call the
  same frames.body_to_local_ned in their own loop, after the Shield (a lab
  decision under ARCH-04). The adapter's MISSION_ITEM_INT row is flown since 3 Nov (mission style;

## 4. Results by work package

One subsection per WP, in the grant's order, each with: what the grant
asks; what was delivered, with the measured numbers and the evidence file;
the deviations; what remains. Appendix A holds the per-obligation table
the subsections are written from.

- 4.1 WP1 Policy DSL and IR: 25 obligations. The IR with a spatial index,
  fences, corridors, time windows, breach actions, priorities, layered
  merge, ingest tools and the round-trip KPI are DONE, and so are the
  KPI-grade replayable bundle (WP1-18, hil_smoke_03), the DSL benches on
  the AGX Orin (WP1-19), the CityLife bundles from a clean tree (WP1-20)
  and the IR migration register (WP1-22). ConstraintBase
  requires the grant's four fields since IR schema 4.0.0 (6 Nov; scope was
  the last, WP1-01), and since 2 Nov rings are stored in canonical order and
  the lint refuses hard corridors no flight can be inside (WP1-02). The
  mid-term Policy DSL + IR is delivered (WP1-21). Since 3 Nov every shipped policy is stored and hashed in WGS84
  with a lazily computed local view, and since 18-22 Nov every flight
  loader refuses a policy stored in local metres (a declared read of a
  policy the lock records excepted; WP1-14, DONE 26 Nov after the
  independent review of the third fix, its dissent closed by a fourth;
  since 26 Nov the DSL loaders, REST, gRPC HotApply, the Ingest CLI and
  the Shield's events refuse metre input too: a tool that works in metres
  converts it first with models.wgs84_from_metres, and the stress harness
  converts its scenario events' metre points before they reach a DSL
  entry); since 27 Nov the bundles are signed under the Lab CA, a lab
  decision of 27 Nov (authority lab-ca, the 17 flight bundles re-signed
  with policy_hash unchanged, the chain required on hil and flight, flown
  in ca1127_001 and in the release 0.7.2 smoke smREL072, 52 KPI-grade
  runs; WP1-04 DONE after the review, 3 of 3; the KPI runs of
  record flew before it, under the bare lab-dev keys);
  distance_envelope is enforced for buildings and roads (flown on hil,
  de_hil2) and, since 19 Nov, every person the rail reports (WP1-07). Open:
  compatibility with the PI's reference implementation (WP1-05),
  people kept at the minimum distance only when perceived (the person
  feed matched 0.189 of pedestrians within 25 m on pas_hil_people_02;
  WP1-07).
- 4.2 WP2 Prefix Compiler: 19 obligations. The CSP model and its schema
  (since 29 Oct it accepts the spec's own example), budget, coverage,

  Every city follow writes its CSP since 28 Oct

## 6. VLA backend measurement set (ARCH-30, X-04)

The grant asks for a Q2 measurement set before a default VLA backend is
chosen. What exists is the follow comparison on Project AirSim CityLife
(simple_flight, so outside the ArduPilot architecture and never KPI
evidence): every model flown pure-VLA and hierarchical (the VLA chooses the
intent, the hand-written controller flies it), on the desktop and on the
Jetson Orin Nano, 27-29 Oct, scored by the share of the mission within 30 m
of the car (pure VLA from its first acquisition). Runs 27-29 Oct
(`demo/out/cmp_*`, `cmp2_*`, `rep3_*` to `rep5_*`, `orin_*`); n is the
number of flights, std the sample standard deviation:

| Platform | Mode | Model | n | Mean | Std | Min-max |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PC | controller | fixed 12 m stand-off (no VLA) | 1 | 0.999 | - | 0.999 |
| PC | controller | hand-written follow (no VLA) | 5 | 0.972 | 0.012 | 0.954-0.983 |
| PC | hierarchical | DroneCATS-2B | 5 | 0.933 | 0.006 | 0.928-0.940 |
| PC | hierarchical | AerialVLA FT r2 | 2 | 0.917 | 0.002 | 0.915-0.918 |
| PC | hierarchical | AerialVLA FT r3 | 3 | 0.897 | 0.034 | 0.869-0.934 |
| PC | hierarchical | AerialVLA FT r1 | 3 | 0.882 | 0.003 | 0.879-0.884 |
| PC | hierarchical | AerialVLA FT r4 | 2 | 0.882 | 0.013 | 0.873-0.891 |
| PC | hierarchical | AerialVLA (original) | 5 | 0.854 | 0.305 | 0.309-0.999 |
| PC | hierarchical | RaceVLA-7B | 2 | 0.720 | 0.001 | 0.719-0.721 |
| PC | pure VLA | AerialVLA (original) | 2 | 0.346 | 0.273 | 0.153-0.539 |
| PC | pure VLA | DroneCATS-2B | 2 | 0.109 | 0.033 | 0.086-0.132 |
| PC | pure VLA | RaceVLA-7B | 2 | 0.091 | 0.011 | 0.084-0.099 |
| PC | pure VLA | AerialVLA FT r1-r4 | 1 each | 0.018-0.065 | - | - |
| Jetson | hierarchical | AerialVLA (original) | 1 | 0.989 | - | - |
| Jetson | controller | hand-written follow (no VLA) | 1 | 0.976 | - | - |
| Jetson | hierarchical | DroneCATS-2B | 1 | 0.969 | - | - |
| Jetson | hierarchical | AerialVLA FT r1-r4 | 1 each | 0.876-0.924 | - | - |
| Jetson | controller | fixed 12 m stand-off (no VLA) | 1 | 0.799 | - | - |
| Jetson | hierarchical | RaceVLA-7B | 1 | 0.704 | - | - |
| Jetson | pure VLA | all seven models | 1 each | 0.017-0.113 | - | - |

How to read it. AerialVLA original, hierarchical, scored 0.979-0.999 in
four of its five PC flights and 0.309 in one (`cmp2_hier_aerovla`), where
the detector had no box on the car for 2,921 of 4,169 ticks after a corner
and the aircraft ended 99.6 m behind on average; its median is 0.987. In
hierarchical mode the VLA's only lever while the car is locked is the
stand-off (12 m at fwd 5 to 22 m at fwd 0), and the score falls with the
stand-off, so a fixed 12 m controller is the ceiling on the PC; the VLA's
own search flew 0-11 % of the ticks (AerialVLA original, PC). No VLA beats the hand-written
controller consistently, and the fine-tuned AerialVLA runs (r1-r4) sit
below the original in both modes.

On the grant's architecture (16-17 Nov, deploy/evidence/vla_backend_table.md,
protocol 2): the AGX Orin column holds R1 for the stub, bc_v3 and
OpenVLA-7B bf16 (p95 969 ms) and server benches for the backends served
over protocol v2; C2, the follow on `hil` (Project AirSim CityLife on
ArduPilot SITL, the companion on the AGX Orin), has 3 flights of every
backend that can fly it, P0 escapes 0 on every flight. Within 30 m of the
car: AIoT-VLA v10b 0.211-0.352, DroneCATS-2B 0.132-0.189, AerialVLA bf16
0.052-0.163, RaceVLA 0.060-0.168; C2's null, the perception node's
box-servo (no estimator, no lock), 0.065-0.111 as flown. No C1 flight
exists. Default backend: AIoT-VLA v10b for the follow task, named by the
lab on 18 Nov on the table, after the set.

## 7. Deviations register

Each deviation, the grant text it departs from, and its state:

| Item | Grant | What we do | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| WP1-25, WP2-19, X-06 | Python 3.11+ | the AGX Orin companion runs 3.12.3 on every `hil` flight; since 14 Nov the desktop's non-VLA side runs 3.12.16 (guardrail-py312: the full CI at 8a473fa, docs/data/ci_local/8a473fa0172e-py312.json; hs10 planned, driven and scored by it; the PAS launcher's scene / check / g1 steps); vla-real 3.10.20 stays for the VLA runtime (torch, transformers) | PI said follow the grant (6 Oct); the grant scopes 3.11+ to the non-VLA stack (docs/DESIGN-python-versions.md): one PAS scene step on 3.12 left, and the PI to confirm that reading |
| WP1-14 | WGS84 canonical, AGL default, ENU/NED computed lazily; the DSL never carries projected coordinates | since 3 Nov every shipped policy is stored and hashed in WGS84 with a stated origin (policies/ holds 33: 27 with points, all lat/lon, and 6 point-free), the local metres a lazy per-origin view (IR 3.1.0), MSL an opt-in with a ground; since 18-22 Nov every flight loader, the six legacy AirSim scripts included, refuses a policy stored in local metres (bundle.load_for_flight); since 26 Nov (fix/wgs84-residuals, four fixes) every Policy DSL entry refuses a point given in local metres or with x/y beside lat/lon (ProjectedCoordinatesRefused, HTTP 422), with the two qualifications at the end of this cell: the DSL loaders (models.load_policy / policy_from_raw / load_layered), the REST bodies and gRPC HotApply (top level and inside `geometry:`), the Ingest CLI (tools/geo_to_policy.py ingest, WP1-16; it converted such input itself until the second fix), the Shield's events and a new bundle.derive; bundle.load_bundle(runtime=True) refuses a metre or x/y-beside bundle as load_for_flight does; the published schema offers {lat, lon} only; a tool that works in metres converts them itself first (models.wgs84_from_metres). The harness's ScenarioEvent (scenario YAML, the hil rail's --events, gRPC EmitEvent) is the Stress Testing harness's own scenario type, not a DSL entry: the grant gives its payload as `dict # event-specific`, in metres relative to the vehicle in its worked example; the harness converts any absolute metre vertices or centerline to WGS84 (scenario_events.in_wgs84, harness_service.rest_body_of_event) before they reach a DSL entry, which takes lat/lon only. The qualifications: (1) a declared legacy read (VLAGUARD_LEGACY_READ=1, or legacy_read) by load_for_flight, load_yaml_for_flight or load_bundle(runtime=True) still reads a policy stored in metres or with x/y beside lat/lon, but since the third fix only a recorded one: one form of its policy_hash, as the era of its document computes it, must be in policies/policy.lock.json (bundle.recorded_as), and any other is refused with the variable set too; since the fourth fix a document in metres or with x/y beside lat/lon matches only a lock record whose own document was in such a form (bundle.projected_era_keys: locked before 6 Oct, or proved metre by the frame migration; wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.0 is one of them although written in WGS84, as its v2 hash digests its projected metres, so its metre rendering still reads as that version, points equal to the bit), so the never-written metre or x/y-beside rendering of a version the lock records in WGS84 only is refused too; (2) the deprecated POST /nfz checks every point but ignores non-point body keys it does not define, while PATCH /dynamic_nfz move refuses any key but op, vertices, motion and layer since the third fix (until then it dropped a `geometry:` block beside lat/lon vertices; nothing projected was stored) and translate, rotate and scale any key but their own since the fourth (until then they answered 200 and dropped it; nothing projected was stored) | retired 26 Nov (second and third fixes); Appendix A, WP1-14, DONE 26 Nov (independent review, 2 of 3): the metre input form this deviation recorded is refused at every Policy DSL entry, the Ingest CLI included; the declared legacy read takes only content the lock records (third fix): an edited, re-versioned or new metre document or bundle is refused with the variable set, and since the fourth fix so is a metre or x/y-beside rendering of a version the lock records in WGS84 only; and the one metre payload left, the stress harness's ScenarioEvent, is the harness's own type, converted before any DSL entry (argued in the previous cell); no PI decision was taken or is claimed. The dissent (1 PARTIAL): bundle.recorded_as matched the lock's pre-6-Oct hash forms against every entry, those of versions locked since in WGS84 included, so a never-written metre rendering of such a version (e.g. fase3-sim-demo@0.1.4) read under VLAGUARD_LEGACY_READ=1; held not to block DONE by the rule that only a confirmed bypass letting a new projected point fly does, as its geometry, origin and floats must equal the recorded policy's to the bit; closed since by the fourth 26 Nov fix (Appendix A, WP1-14). Earlier: the review of the second fix 2 DONE, 1 PARTIAL, kept PARTIAL (the declared legacy read flew a new metre YAML); the re-review of the first 1 DONE, 2 PARTIAL (the Ingest CLI's conversion, the ScenarioEvent wording, load_bundle) |
| WP1-01 | ConstraintBase: constraint_type, scope, priority, violation_action required | since IR schema 3.0.0 (2 Nov) constraint_type, priority and violation_action are required and the action is one of the grant's six ('repair' is read as project_fix); since IR schema 4.0.0 (6 Nov) `scope` too: a rule without it is refused at every DSL entry, every shipped rule states `scope: mission` (scope follows the authoring layer, and every shipped rule is a mission-layer rule), and a stored document of an earlier era is read in its own era, scope absent, so it re-derives its recorded hash | closed 6 Nov: conformed (IR bump), no waiver (docs/IR-MIGRATIONS.md, the 4.0.0 row) |
| ARCH-15 | the HIL bridge carries "AirSim sensor messages (`HIL_GPS`, `HIL_SENSOR`)" into AP SITL (04-risks-and-fallbacks/fallback-gates.md, G1 condition 1) | Project AirSim's ArduCopterApi JSON sensor frames over UDP 9003 to ArduPilot's SIM_AirSim backend, PWM back over UDP 9002; G1's four conditions passed on this transport (demo/out/g1_20261008_223259, g1_20261013_203404; demo/pas_ardupilot/g1_check.py), first on 8 Oct, after the gate's 30 June date; the perception rail is kept on Project AirSim and the Colosseum swap was not made | recorded deviation: the transport, not the bridge's function, and the gate's swap rule not applied; ArduCopter 4.5.7 has no HIL_SENSOR handler and takes HIL_GPS only as a MAVLink GPS (docs/FINAL-REPORT.md section 7.1) |

## 9. Decisions needed from the PI

Settled on 2 Nov (the researcher, with the PI's standing instruction of
6 Oct to follow the grant and use best practice where it is silent):

- theta basis: forecast depth in metres, the shipped default (Q1);
- fail-safe rubric, IR bump on every shape change (WP1-22), two-hash
  contract (WP1-05), multi-scale geometry in the bundle (WP2-16), the DRIFT
  items: best practice, conforming to the grant rather than asking for
  waivers;
- Python: the version every environment supports, otherwise the best one
  with the reason recorded (WP1-25, X-06);
- the five final-demo scenarios: after the VLA and the system are mature;
- the WP2 / WP4 correction notes (WP2-12, WP4-21): sent to ITRI;
- `hil` fallback date: as soon as the AGX Orin is installed. The work
  stops at `hil`: the drone cannot carry an AGX Orin or run a large VLA, so
  the `flight` topology (ARCH-14) still needs the PI's written waiver.

Settled on 17 Nov by the project. These rows are reported on what was
delivered and where it is; Appendix A moves them on that basis
(docs/FINAL-REPORT.md section 9, decisions 4 and 5):

- dated deliverables (WP1-21, WP3-01, ARCH-15, ARCH-16, ARCH-17, ARCH-23):
  reported on what was delivered and where it is; ARCH-15 stays DRIFT on
  its transport and the gate's swap rule;
- team (ARCH-24): stated as in the grant, 1 PI + 4 Master students, one
  team.

Decided on 22 Nov by the user, for the lab (not the PI's decisions;
docs/FINAL-REPORT.md section 9):

- wait for the PI to name the CA: no key certified, nothing signed under
  a CA (the 25 Nov changelog rebuild re-signed with the same lab-dev keys)
  (WP1-04, X-10, X-01); superseded on 27 Nov by the user's decision, for

Decided on 27 Nov by the user, for the lab (not the PI's decision):

No PI or ITRI acceptance of the Lab CA is recorded (the grant notes the choice "Affects deployment"); the move to ITRI's CA is written and tested but not done. The KPI runs of record (ntFIN4, ntFIN5, smREL070, smREL071) flew before 27 Nov under the bare lab-dev keys, with chain none and require_chain false (smREL071: 52 of 52 runs); the Gazebo mid-term gate (dev) flew a lab-dev-signed bundle (docs/data/gazebo_midterm_20261125/); under the CA have flown only ca1127_001 (one smoke cell, one seed) and the release 0.7.2 smoke smREL072 (52 runs, one seed per cell, not a figure of record), and the PAS perception rail, the dev rails and Gazebo have not; flight.env's 1 has never flown (ARCH-14). Custody: the CA private key is an unencrypted file on the lab desktop, valid 10 years, and there is no CRL or OCSP (retiring or removing a trust-store entry is the only mechanism, and the certified leaves certify the same key pairs as the retired bare identities); issued_at is the signer's own word, so retired_on is a naming rule, not revocation, and only require_chain (on for hil and flight, off for dev) refuses a backdated bare-key bundle. The KPI gate does not check the chain: guardrail.manifest.is_kpi_grade checks no signature or chain, and a hil config or a desktop VLAGUARD_REQUIRE_CHAIN=0 overrides hil.env. No hil flight shows the loader refusing a bare-key bundle (CLI rc 1 and tests only). The lab-ca root is the desktop harness's mTLS root; the 3 Nov SROS 2 record names a lab CA fingerprint 69:73:B8:BA... (CHANGELOG 2026-11-03), neither the SHA-1 nor the SHA-256 of this certificate, so one root shared with SROS 2 (Stress Testing: "Same root CA as SROS 2") is not shown. The evidence was made on branch wp1-04-lab-ca and merged into master on 27 Nov (release 0.7.1, a3d616e, ships the bare-key trust store; release 0.7.2, 418b151, ships the Lab CA trust store); local CI at its head 3479613: 191/191 files, 4966 of 4986 tests passed (20 skipped), headless smoke rc 0 (docs/data/ci_local/3479613c52c0.json), and the fastapi files that skip there pass under Python 3.12 (test_api 19/19, test_railinputs_node 25/25, test_railinputs_hotapply 6/6, test_pas_hotapply 9/9, test_harness_service 63/63); the re-signed bundles, the run folder, its replay bundle and video are gitignored (committed: the tool records, the run's JSON files, the trust store). |

A frameless policy refuses every point (NoFrameOrigin, 422 on REST since 18 Nov); gen_random_scenario --metres removed. The harness's ScenarioEvent (scenario YAML events, the hil rail's --events, the gRPC harness's EmitEvent, a network entry of the harness) is the Stress Testing harness's own scenario type, not a Policy DSL entry: the grant (Stress Testing page) defines its payload as `dict # event-specific` and gives its worked example in metres relative to the vehicle (width_m, height_m, ahead_of_vehicle_m, mps). Our stress library may also give a spawn's vertices or a swap's centerline as absolute local metres; the harness converts them to WGS84 about the flown policy's frame origin (scenario_events.in_wgs84; harness_service.rest_body_of_event for the REST forward) before they reach a DSL entry (a Shield event or a REST / gRPC hot-apply body), which takes lat/lon only. A Shield is built on a policy stored in local metres only under a declared read that covers that content (models.shield_policy_problem, Policy.legacy_read_covers: models.read_recorded_policy, load_legacy_metre_policy, load_for_flight / load_bundle legacy_read, Shield(legacy_metres=True)). tests/test_wgs84_entry_residuals.py 28/28, test_geo_to_policy.py 27/27, test_wgs84_writes.py 32/32, test_wgs84_ingest_writers.py 12/12, test_wgs84_frameless_writers.py 12/12, test_legacy_rails_wgs84_only.py 9/9, test_api.py 19/19 (guardrail-py312). since the second 26 Nov fix bundle.load_bundle(runtime=True) applies the same two refusals (its one caller in the code, write_bundle's `previous`, declares the read); since 22 Nov also the six legacy AirSim scripts (bundle.LEGACY_YAML_RAILS, refuse_or_load_yaml; bundle.recorded_as, the lock read once and cached), and any other is refused (LegacyPointFormRefused, naming models.wgs84_from_metres or the WGS84 successor bundle) with the variable set too; the 13 legacy lab bundles and ten historical policies/*.yaml versions taken from git read under it; a metre YAML or bundle with new content (a vertex moved, a rule added, a version or issued_at changed) does not, and since the fourth 26 Nov fix neither does the never-written metre or x/y-beside rendering of a version the lock records in WGS84 only (it reproduces that version's legacy 16-hex forms, which the lock keeps for every entry): a document in metres or with x/y beside lat/lon matches only a lock record whose own document was in such a form, decided from the lock's fields (bundle.projected_era_keys: an entry with no hash_scheme of its own, locked up to 6 Oct under the v2 scheme, which digested the projected metres; an entry a later one names in migrated_from with migration.old_hash_form sha256-canonical-v3/local-metres, the frame migration of 3 Nov, and the migrated_from chain below it; a derived recipe on either), and an entry recorded in WGS84 only is matched by its own canonical hash only, which stores lat/lon; with the fourth fix the 13 lab bundles, the 53 rewound metre versions of the lock (bundle.policy_candidates) and every policies/*.yaml version in git read as before; the read is recorded as legacy_read/point_form/recorded_as (and projected_points). tools/bundle_census.py (docs/data/bundle_census_20261118/): 13 legacy + 4 frameless bundles each mapped to a flight-ready successor; Confirmed by the reviewers: under VLAGUARD_LEGACY_READ=1 the previous round's new metre YAML is refused (LegacyPointFormRefused, "not a recorded policy") by load_for_flight and load_yaml_for_flight, and so are the recorded fase3-sim-demo@0.1.2 metre YAML with a vertex moved 1 m or 50 m, a metre rule added, its version or issued_at changed, an origin added or scope stated, a mixed WGS84 + metre document, a fresh metre bundle (load_for_flight, load_bundle(runtime=True); read with runtime=False it is refused at Shield construction), a pre-6-Oct-format bundle of a new policy and PolicyRef.load_with_source (gRPC InjectPolicy); the unedited 0.1.2 YAML and the 13 legacy lab bundles read with recorded_as set and build a Shield; check_bundle recomputes a bundle's hash, so a bundle cannot claim one; update_lock refuses a metre YAML; PATCH move answers 422 to a `geometry:` block, center, origin or x/y; three of the new tests fail on 52ac08b. The dissent (PARTIAL), held not to block DONE by the rule that only a confirmed bypass letting a new projected point fly does: bundle.recorded_as matched a document's pre-6-Oct (legacy16), prior-v2 and IR 2.x hash forms against every lock entry, and the lock keeps the legacy forms for entries locked since (107 of 128), WGS84-only IR 4.0.0 versions included, so the never-written metre rendering, with origin, of a locked WGS84 version (fase3-sim-demo@0.1.4, wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.2, follow-car-citylife-nfz3b@0.1.3, poly-test@0.1.3, corridor-survey@0.1.3; YAML, a write_bundle(allow_legacy_metres=True) bundle, or a hand-built pre-6-Oct-format bundle) read under the variable labelled recorded_as that version and a Shield built on it; its geometry, frame origin and floats had to equal the recorded policy's to the bit, so no new point flew, but the run record named a rendering that was never recorded; likewise a copy of 0.1.4 with x/y written beside lat/lon (x/y off by 5 m: refused). Without the variable every one was refused. Closed by the fourth 26 Nov fix (fix/wgs84-residuals): a document in local metres or with x/y beside lat/lon matches only a lock record whose own document was in such a form, or whose recorded hash digests such a form (bundle.projected_era_keys, from the lock's fields as the evidence cell says; wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.0, written in WGS84 but hashed as projected metres, is the one such entry whose file was not in metres, see below), and an entry recorded in WGS84 only is matched by its own canonical hash only, so each of those renderings, as YAML, bundle and pre-6-Oct-format bundle, is refused under the variable too, the refusal naming the version it reproduces (tests/test_wgs84_entry_residuals.py test_a_projected_rendering_of_a_wgs84_only_version_is_refused, for fase3-sim-demo@0.1.4 and wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.2 through load_for_flight, load_yaml_for_flight and load_bundle(runtime=True); it and the two new PATCH tests (the handler in that file, the route in test_api.py) and the changed test of the legacy16 x/y-beside bundle fail on 6a80730), while the recorded metre versions and the pre-6-Oct x/y-beside bundle of wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.0 still read. Verified 26 Nov by two independent read-only verifications of the fourth fix (791e7f0, 629bead), both closing it: of the 65 lock entries outside projected_era_keys, the 54 with points were rendered in metres (with and without origin, IR 2.x and current views, scope dropped) and with x/y beside lat/lon, each as YAML, as a write_bundle bundle and as pre-6-Oct-format bundles, and loaded with the variable set through load_for_flight, load_yaml_for_flight, load_bundle(runtime=True) and PolicyRef.load_with_source: none was accepted, where 6a80730 accepted 4,590 such loads; a forged policy_hash, hashes entry or legacy form is refused by check_bundle, and a hash field written into a YAML is refused as extra_forbidden; the 56 files of the lab bundles folder and the 266 distinct policies/*.yaml blobs in git history (git log --all) read exactly as on 6a80730; 57 PATCH translate/rotate/scale/move bodies with a key the op does not define, which 6a80730 answered 200 with a new generation, answer 422. One residual of the dissent's kind remains and is disclosed here: wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.0 was written in WGS84 (lat/lon with an origin, projected at load time) but locked on 6 Oct with no hash_scheme of its own, so its v2 policy_hash digests the projected metres and criterion (a) puts it in projected_era_keys; its never-written metre rendering, and a YAML of it with x/y beside lat/lon, read under the variable labelled recorded_as wgs84-taipei-demo@0.1.0, because the lock's record of that version is the hash of exactly those metres and cannot tell the two documents apart; the points must still equal the recorded ones to the bit, so no new point can fly; a point moved 5 m is refused (tests/test_wgs84_entry_residuals.py test_the_pre_6_oct_wgs84_version_reads_in_its_recorded_metres). The verifications also found that a PATCH whose op is not a string (a list) answered 500 instead of 422, nothing applied; since the fix of those findings it answers 422 (guardrail/api.py apply_nfz_edit; tests/test_api.py test_routes_refuse_an_edit_key_the_op_does_not_define). Also closed by the fourth fix: PATCH /dynamic_nfz/{id} translate, rotate and scale ignored keys they do not define and answered 200 with a new generation ({x, y} vertices, a `geometry:` block, a metre center on rotate, which rotates about the vertex mean; nothing projected stored, GET /policy carries no x/y); they now answer 422 naming the key (api.EDIT_KEYS), as move does; their dx/dy, angle and factor are relative edits, as the grant's translate and rotate ScenarioEvents are. Disclosed: the deprecated POST /nfz ignores body keys it does not define (e.g. `center`), where /dynamic_nfz and /hot_apply refuse them (extra_forbidden). Not guarded in process (guardrail/models.py "WHICH LAYER ENFORCES IT"): the type, Policy.model_validate, still reads the legacy metre form and a lat/lon point with x/y beside it (the x/y checked to 1 um and dropped), because every recorded artefact (a run's policy_g<N>.json, an old bundle's ir.json, a lock recipe) is read through it; Shield construction checks the metre forms only and the Prefix Compiler (compiler.ConstraintCompiler) none, so an in-process caller that builds either on a policy it validated itself is not refused. VLAGUARD_LEGACY_READ=1 still declares the legacy read for every load_for_flight and load_bundle in the process, but since the third fix each such read is checked against the lock; bundle.derive(legacy_read=True), the lock recipes' rebuild, is an in-process call and is not. Shield.move_nfz, called in process, keeps a vertex's lat and lon and drops any other key (`alt`, say), where REST answers 422. The harness takes lat/lon over an x/y written beside them in an event payload without checking the x/y (scenario_events._point, rest_body_of_event). Earlier reviews: the re-review of the first fix (b2a644d) 1 DONE, 2 PARTIAL, on the Ingest CLI converting {x, y} at ingest, the ScenarioEvent wording and load_bundle(runtime=True), closed by the second fix with four smaller findings (POST /nfz stored `x: null`, REST dropped unknown point keys, Shield.move_nfz refused {lat, lon} in a models.XY, the schema did not say a null altitude_ref means AGL); the review of the second fix 2 DONE, 1 PARTIAL, kept PARTIAL because under the variable load_for_flight read a new, never-recorded metre YAML and a Shield was built on it, closed by the third fix. The review's suggested hardenings are in the fourth fix: the hash forms of a projected document matched by the era of the lock record (decided from the record's point form, as above), and a key allow-list for PATCH translate, rotate and scale. The 26 Nov refusals are tested, not flown; the 22 Nov legacy-script refusal is tested, not flown (those AirSim scripts are not used by any KPI rail). |

Two deliberate changes (CHANGELOG 2026-11-26): (1) an untouched tick now returns the body action handed in, not its round trip (at most 3.6e-15 m/s apart); (2) at a heading that is not a quarter turn, a NaN horizontal body channel beside a finite one is now zeroed alone and the finite channel flies, where before both went to zero. Change (2) is less conservative; the action-finite violation is still raised (`test_sanitise_runs_on_the_body_action_one_channel_at_a_time`). Residuals: (1) The simulator demo loops (demo/run_demo.py, vla_fly.py, follow_vlm.py and the others listed) call frames.body_to_local_ned in their own loop, after the Shield, not through a MAVLink adapter, because they send Project AirSim velocity commands and have no MAVLink link. They use the one function, and tests/test_frame_boundary.py:915 pins the caller list. This is accepted under ARCH-04 (DONE, independent review 20 Nov), a lab decision, not the PI's. A reader who holds "the conversion lives in the MAVLink adapter" to the letter across all rails would need the PI's acceptance on this point alone. (2) The NaN-channel change (the finite channel now flies) is less conservative than before. It is pinned by a test but has not been flown. (3) The opt-in arc forecast (`--arc-forecast`, off by default) is unflown: a body command with a yaw rate is forecast along a straight world line. (4) An operator registered through repair.register is handed the body action (forward / right), as the grant asks; one written for North / East must convert through ctx.to_world. The profile replays 1,301 ticks from a pack built at 401305abd019-dirty (4 older flights), the as-flown rules topped up to 50 with 44-47 synthetic square no-fly zones; the flown ntFIN5 policies had 3-5 constraints. The 119,087 non-setpoint ntFIN5 ticks, read from their tick_phases_ms on 26 Nov (tools/nonsetpoint_ticks.py, docs/data/ntFIN5_nonsetpoint_ticks/): tick entry to the end of the publish phase, an upper bound of entry to the mode request, p50 6.85 / p99 19.59 / max 41.70 ms; the whole tick p99 22.01 / max 87.76 ms (69.6 ms of that one after its decision); 0 of 119,087 over 100 ms; 758 ticks carried an FSM SET_MODE: the 281 that published no setpoint took at most 41.70 ms to the end of the publish phase, and the other 477 published a setpoint, so they are in the 0 of 183,961 (tick_ms max 45.86 ms). VLA-in-loop hil ticks, on the PAS perception rail outside the KPI campaigns: pab_aerialvla_04 (19-20 Nov, bfb4973, AerialVLA = OpenVLA-7B + aero_vla LoRA bf16 and OWL-ViT on the AGX Orin; 12 flights, KPI-grade as flown, 0 KPI-grade under the single_sha gate of 27 Nov: single_sha pending, the PAS script outside the harness never added the desktop's revision) 4 of 27,582 ticks over 100 ms, max 142.85 ms, tick p99 47.2 ms, the Shield step itself 71.5-85.3 ms on each on a clearance repair (docs/data/kpi_prefix_vla_20261120/README.md, 'Ticks over 100 ms on this rail'; re-counted from the 12 flight logs on 26 Nov); pas_hil_feed_07 (15 Nov, deac4b3, AIoT-VLA) 3 of 4,058 over (152.5, 111.6, 147.9 ms; CHANGELOG 2026-11-15 (10 Hz observation)). | The two numbers are met at release 0.7.0 (21c2771). Geometry query: _check p99 1.056 ms, max 1.100 ms against 5 ms at 50 rules x 51 poses. Flown tick: node e2e_ms max 62.18 ms, 0 of 183,961 setpoint ticks over 100 ms in ntFIN5. The grant's measurement conditions are not met, for five reasons. (1) The grant's hil topology (topologies.md) is "the configuration every reported KPI is measured against", with the Orin running "MAVROS 2, the Suffix Shield ROS 2 node, and the full VLA backend". Every ntFIN5 and smREL070 run uses a stub pilot from guardrail.vla_stub (GotoPilot, ConstantPilot or StubVLA, per the KPI reports' episode model field), and the 25 Nov profile ran with no VLA server. The tick under VLA load on the Orin at 0.7.0 is unmeasured. No VLA-loaded tick has been measured on the ROS 2 Shield node at 0.7.0; the VLA-in-loop hil ticks on record are on the PAS perception rail at earlier revisions and include ticks over 100 ms (see (5)); the AIoT-VLA camera-to-first-setpoint figure (14 Nov, p99 282-296 ms) is a different quantity. (2) The ~50-rule design load was never flown. The ntFIN5 policies had 3-5 constraints. The 50-rule figure is an offline replay of the Shield step only. 44-47 of the 50 rules are synthetic square no-fly zones added along the path. The tick pack was built at 401305abd019-dirty. The profile's whole filter step reaches 51.06 ms max, so a flown 50-rule tick within 100 ms is inferred, not shown. (3) "End to end" is read as the pilot's action to the /mavros/setpoint_raw/local topic. The MAVROS-to-autopilot MAVLink hop is not timed (the profile's not_measured field says so). The 119,087 ntFIN5 ticks that published no setpoint, among them 281 of the 758 SET_MODE ticks, have no tick_ms or e2e_ms and are outside the 0-of-183,961 count (the other 477 SET_MODE ticks published a setpoint and are inside it, tick_ms max 45.86 ms); read from their tick_phases_ms they were all within 100 ms (max 41.70 ms to the end of the publish phase, 87.76 ms whole; docs/data/ntFIN5_nonsetpoint_ticks/), but the hops after the node (the adapter, MAVROS, the autopilot) are not timed for them either. (4) The bag hops pair each output with the latest input before it. Actions come every ~100 ms, so a hop over about 101 ms would pair with the next action and read short. The bag result corroborates the node's per-action e2e_ms (tracked by action_seq) but cannot fail on its own. (5) Learned-VLA hil flights outside the KPI campaigns did exceed 100 ms: pab_aerialvla_04 4 of 27,582 ticks (max 142.85 ms; the Shield step 71.5-85.3 ms on a clearance repair with OpenVLA-7B and OWL-ViT sharing the Orin, cause open) and pas_hil_feed_07 3 of 4,058. They ran on the PAS perception rail, not on the ROS 2 Shield node the KPI campaigns fly, but until that cause is found a VLA-loaded run is not shown to meet the budget. Also: the profile ran with clocks not locked (no sudo) and MAVROS idle with no FCU. The KPI JSON's period_over_budget (41,380) counts 10 Hz periods of 101-102 ms: jitter, not tick latency. ntFIN4's 105.5 ms tick at 59e19a5 led to the hot-apply fix and stays in the record. To close the row, either: a KPI-grade hil run at 0.7.0 with a learned VLA served on the same AGX and a ~50-rule bundle flown, timing non-setpoint and escalation ticks too; or a recorded PI/lab decision that the stub pilot and the offline 50-rule replay are acceptable for this KPI, which is put to the PI (section 9.2, question (g)). |

| WP4-11 | Every episode produces a self-contained bundle replayable bit-for-bit: manifest.json, policy_bundle (incl. all hot-apply generations), episode.bag2 (rosbag2), shield_audit.jsonl, harness_events.jsonl, kpi.json; Q4 reports Q3 runs 'replayed for reproducibility' | PARTIAL | Headless episodes re-fly bit-for-bit (tools/rerun_episode.py, CHANGELOG 2026-10-13). Since 7 Nov every flown hil run has held a signed `<run>.replay.tar.gz`. Since 18-19 Nov each rail-campaign episode has also been written under the grant's six names: manifest.json, policy_bundle.tar.gz, episode.bag2/, shield_audit.jsonl, harness_events.jsonl and kpi.json. Every episode of the hil campaigns of record records all six as written in kpi.json `bundle_files`. ntFIN5: 654/654 (21c2771, demo/out/hil_ntFIN5/episode-*). Each manifest.json holds the six determinism fields (code_revision 21c277170001, vla_model_hash, policy_hash, random_seed, sim_speedup 1.0, topology hil), and each episode.bag2/ holds bag_0.mcap and metadata.yaml. smREL070: 52/52. smREL071: 52/52 (a3d616e, 26 Nov, docs/data/kpi_smREL071/). Before these, ntFIN4 had 654/654 and smFIN2 52/52. The pdyn1126 episodes and the harness-driven wgs01_001 episode also wrote the six names. policy_bundle.tar.gz carries the hot-apply generations: a pdyn1126 corridor-swap-narrowed episode holds generations/policy_g0.json, generations/policy_g1.json and injected/corridor-survey-v0.1.3.tar.gz, with bundle_files 'written, complete' (every generation's IR hash-checked, CHANGELOG 2026-11-22 (code)). Replay at the flown revision: tools/replay_at_revision.py builds a detached worktree at the manifest's code_revision and runs open loop on the logged inputs. On the AGX Orin, inside the hil companion container with the Shield node's runtime (aarch64, Python 3.12.3, numpy 1.26.4), all 654 ntFIN5 episodes are identical, 0 differ and 0 are not replayable (303,048 ticks, 191,106 audit records, 223.6 s; docs/data/replay_agx_ntFIN5/, 26 Nov). In 228 runs a hot-apply generation is installed mid-run, with its hash checked. What is compared: shield_audit.jsonl records except `ts` and the node's fsm_* fields, and per flight-log row raw, violations, repairs, touched, braked and emitted, bit for bit. The replay's heading input is the audit's full double on 105,932 ticks and the log's 0.01-degree value on 111,942 ticks (per-run `limits`). The same replay on the desktop (AMD64 Windows, Python 3.10.20; docs/data/kpi_ntFIN5/replay_at_revision_ntFIN5.json): 352 identical and 302 differ. Of the 302, 192 differ in the last digits of the body-frame rotation and 109 in other float last digits (emitted action at most 6.9e-14). One differs in a decision: ntFIN5_306 dyn-nfz-rotate-spin, repair_attempts at tick 33, the cell that also differed as ntFIN4_304. That run is identical on the Orin. smREL070 on the desktop: 43 identical, 9 differ in last digits (7 body-frame, 2 float), 0 not replayable (docs/data/kpi_smREL070/). smREL071 on the desktop: 44 identical, 8 differ in the last float digits (5 through the body-frame rotation, 3 plain), 0 not replayable (docs/data/kpi_smREL071/replay_at_revision_smREL071.json). pdyn1126 on the desktop: 9 identical, 3 differ in the last float digits (2 plain, 1 through the body-frame rotation), 0 not replayable (docs/data/kpi_prefix_dyn_20261126/replay_at_revision_pdyn1126.json). Check from the bag alone (tools/replay_from_bag.py, docs/data/replay_from_bag_ntFIN5/; this does not re-run the Shield): manifest_in_bag and trajectory are identical in 654/654. Ticks and KPI are identical in 630/654; the 24 that differ are the 24 gps-noise-beyond-margin runs, at the escalation tick's inferred setpoint. Q3: the replay at revision of the 1 Aug-31 Oct window (docs/data/replay_at_revision_20261118.md) re-flew 720 of the 5,509 Q3 episodes at their clean commit, and all 720 are identical. These are the headless sweep/ (fb8f87c) and sweep_cap_20261029/ (d9ba810) episodes, compared on harness events and KPI only; no audit record or flight-log tick was compared. 4,744 were refused (4,724 flown from a -dirty tree, 20 unversioned) and 45 are not replayable. Separately, tools/replay_q3.py (17 Nov) re-ran 15 Q3 rail runs through the Shield as it stood on 17 Nov (10 identical, 5 differ). That is a regression check: none of the 15 was flown from a clean revision. | Bit-for-bit is shown for one campaign, on the runtime it flew on, and only open loop. No hil or SITL episode is re-flown closed loop with its trajectory reproduced; SITL timing and the network are not deterministic (docs/data/replay_agx_ntFIN5/README.md). The comparison leaves out the audit's `ts` and fsm_* (escalation FSM) fields, and on 111,942 ticks the heading input is the log's 0.01-degree value, not the flown double. The replay is not bit-for-bit across hosts: 302 of 654 ntFIN5 replays differ on the desktop, which runs another CPU, OS and Python, and 1 of them differs in a decision. 'Every episode' is not shown: smREL071 and pdyn1126 are replayed only on the desktop (44/52 and 9/12 identical, the rest in the last float digits), not on the Orin; wgs01_001 has no replay record; ntFIN4 has none on the Orin; and the 132 Gazebo sweep episodes of 24-25 Nov record code_revision `unversioned` (fixed at 6a78190), so they cannot be replayed at their revision. Q4's 'full KPI matrix from Q3 runs replayed for reproducibility' is not done. The KPI matrix of record comes from Q4 runs (ntFIN5, flown 23 Nov). Only 720 of the 5,509 Q3 episodes replay at their revision, all headless and compared on events and KPI only. The 4,744 flown from uncommitted trees cannot be replayed and would have to be flown again. Two decisions are open and not recorded: whether open-loop replay meets 'replayed bit-for-bit', and whether a Q4 campaign replayed at its revision can stand in for the Q3 runs. Both are put to the PI (section 9.2, question (i)). |

The per-build half (GitHub and tools/ci_local.py) flies only the 64-episode headless smoke, which is functional and never KPI-grade. The release's own GitHub runs (45156163225 master, 45156162082 tag) failed in 'Standalone test files' before the smoke step. Release 0.7.1's own runs (45762447982 master, 45762446649 tag) failed too, in the 3.11-extras job's 'Standalone test files' (two stale tests sending metre hot-apply bodies, which 0.7.1's WP1-14 change refuses with HTTP 422), that job's smoke skipped. Release 0.7.2's own runs (45969020953 master, 45969019967 tag) are green, the smoke in all 3 jobs. The first green GitHub smoke, 45497539573, is on 339cacf, 21 commits after the release. To state beside it, from smREL070: High-speed near-edge: 039-040 asked for 20 m/s, but kin-caps in policy fase3-sim-demo (4.0 m/s) clamped all 242 audited ticks, so they flew at most 4.06 m/s. The near-edge flights at speed are near-edge-at-12mps 041-042, at about 10.3 m/s. Three simultaneous violations: 043 triggered RTL with P0 escape 0 but fails its scenario gates. Time window: of the six runs, 023, 024 and 025 fail their gates. time-window-switch-event 027 switches the rule by a hot-applied deactivate_rule, not by a clock-boundary crossing. GPS noise: the noise is applied to the Shield's and the FSM's pose on the companion. The autopilot's EKF sees clean GPS. Seeds: each cell flies once on seed 1001. What closes the row: the next release cut under docs/RUNBOOK-release.md with its own five-row KPI-grade smoke kept (0.7.1 and 0.7.2 kept their smokes; their step 1 records cover the lab checkout only). The report states it plainly (section 10): the five-row matrix has run in full, KPI-grade, at every release from 0.7.0 on; 0.6.0 ran four of five and 0.1.0-0.5.1 predate the smoke profile; the PI is asked to accept that reading (section 9.2, question (c)). For (2), the grant's R9 mitigation runs the smoke set at sim_speedup 1.0 nightly (04-risks-and-fallbacks/risks.md:142), which the per-release KPI-grade smoke plus the per-push headless smoke is not either. |

The only clean prefix-on pass is the stationary spawn (dyn-nfz-spawn-ahead 2/2). Clock-driven changes stay refused for the consumer: the template's worked example dyn-nfz-translate (a moving zone) and windowed time_window_switch (experiments/sweep_scenarios.py:3047 prefix_consumer_refusal; prefix_dyn.yaml:44-68). The profile calls itself 'not a grant scheduling row' (grant_kpi_bearing false) and says part of any on/off delta 'holds by construction' (prefix_dyn.yaml:72-80). No flight carries all four components with an event sent over the grant's REST hot-apply endpoint (Stress_Testing.txt:24-27). In pdyn1126 the Shield node applied each event from --events (harness_events.jsonl events_source). A REST or gRPC hot-apply to a consumer episode is refused by design (guardrail/harness_service.py:2085 _consumer_takes_no_events; sweep_scenarios.py prefix_generation_problems). wgs01_001 was REST-driven but had no CSP consumer. corridor_swap with the prefix on failed 2/2 (pdyn1126_005, _007). At the swap (t=3.047 s) the aircraft was at y=4.03 moving at 2.0 m/s, about 0.97 m inside the new edge. It left the P0 corridor route-narrow about 0.4-0.6 s later in GUIDED under FSM Brake: the Shield passed the consumer's turned action, then escaped with vy -1.8 to -1.96. Peak depth was 0.81 m (_005) and 0.64 m (_007) at t about 4.3 s (max_offset_from_corridor 5.806 against a 5 m half-width). The prefix-off arms leave the corridor too (_006 reached 0.69 m out), but in autopilot Loiter (flown=false), which the scorer does not count. So 'P0 escape 0' is the scorer's emitted-action measure, not 'never outside a P0 rule'. radius-scaling-grow with the prefix on ended RTL_triggered 2/2. Prefix-on mission success is 2/6. The pilot reading the CSP is hand-written. No learned VLA has flown a suite scenario in the harness. Evidence: docs/data/kpi_prefix_dyn_20261126/ holds the re-score, the replay at revision and the bag hops (committed 704b656) and the README (aabfd8e); the per-run kpi.json and manifests stay on the offload drive. The stored hil_pdyn1126/sweep.json still says headless-kinematic and kpi_grade_episodes 0 (written before a44d636, not rewritten); the KPI report of 27 Nov reads the runs (12 KPI-grade). Whether the five scenarios of section 9.1 plus pdyn1126 meet the gate is put to the PI (section 9.2, question (k)). |

Where the gate record lives The record is docs/data/gazebo_midterm_20261125/ (README.md, gate_check.json). The run folders under demo/out/ are gitignored and kept on the lab disk. The signed bundle file under bundles/ is not committed. How the gate was flown The pilot is the rails' StubVLA, which the grant's dev topology allows, and it reads no text. The Shield node parses the task text, which names the northeast pad at (30, 30). The scenario's target moves the pad to (45, 12), so the flown path is set by the scenario, not by the text. The bundle is signed with a lab development key and has no certificate chain, because no CA has been named. The bundle has two envelope rules (alt-band, kin-caps); the gate asks for one. The FSM enters Brake on tick 1 and stays there for the rest of the mission, while the repaired pilot action keeps being flown. kin-caps clamps 6 m/s to 4 m/s on every tick. The videos are flight-log replay renders, not screen captures of Gazebo. All Gazebo runs are functional_only, not KPI-grade. Earlier flights on this rail In the 2 Nov flights with the Shield on, the projection was abandoned (repair 8.14 m against theta 2 m) and the FSM escalated to Loiter and then RTL, so the target was not reached. WP3-15 addresses this false trigger. 20 Nov functional sweep at 28a594d: 8 missions on one seed, 7 passed (docs/data/gazebo_sweep_20261120/). 24-25 Nov gazebo_functional profile at release 0.7.0: 132 missions on 3 seeds, P0 escape 0. ## Independent-review note (13 Nov 2026: the rows against the nightly `hil` campaign ntFIN2)

Re-assessed against the grant pages' own words (kuanting-vla-uav-guardrail/docs/,
each row quoting the line it is held to), not reviewed by anyone outside
the project. It records what moved, why, and what an external reviewer
could still dispute.

**What changed.** Eight rows from PARTIAL to DONE on the KPI-grade nightly
ntFIN2 (116214f, 580 KPI-grade episodes, tools/kpi_report.py --campaign):
WP3-14 (P0 escape 0 over 16,703 P0-violation ticks, Shield-off control
0.6375); the KPIs the grant lists as tracked, each now with a KPI-grade
value - WP3-16 (6.407 m/s), WP3-17 (3.23 s), WP3-20 and X-02 (98.2 %),
X-03 (55.4 per mission), WP4-08 (252/577); and WP4-12 (the six fields on
all 583 runs at one clean revision, sim_speedup 1.0, hil). Counts 70 / 59 /
6 / 1 become 78 / 51 / 6 / 1. Evidence refreshed with no status change:
WP3-07, WP3-13, WP3-15, WP3-19, WP4-01, WP4-04, WP4-06, WP4-11, WP4-14,
WP4-17, ARCH-19, ARCH-22, ARCH-31, X-14, and the open notes of WP2-15,
WP3-08, WP3-11.

**Why the rest stay PARTIAL.** WP3-15 (DONE 15 Nov on the backstop rule,
584/585): until then 0.998 held on the 567 runs scored with the supplement
ntFIN2s (566/567) and 21 were unscored (19 censored in Loiter, 2 cell-seeds
with no accepted flight); 3 remain unscored, and were all 3 errors it would
be 584/588 = 0.993. WP3-13 (DONE 14 Nov): 13 start-up ticks over 100 ms
in ntFIN2; the fix then flew in hs09 and in the full campaign ge1113 with no
tick over 100 ms. WP4-17 and ARCH-19: the KPI report is committed from a
clean tree since 13 Nov evening (docs/data/kpi_ntFIN2); the final report is
a draft (docs/FINAL-REPORT.md), not signed or delivered. WP4-06, WP4-14, WP3-19: per build and per release need a
push and a release, and GPS noise / latency has no rail. WP4-01: the
paraphrase and prefix KPIs have `hil` values only from text-reading pilots
flown outside the stress harness. WP4-04: the multi-seed functional sweep
flown 24-25 Nov (132 missions on 3 seeds, after one seed on 20 Nov, 7 of 8);
no speed > 1.
WP4-11: the six bundle names are written on every hil campaign episode of record since 22 Nov, but the replay is open loop only (ntFIN5 654/654 on the AGX Orin), no episode is re-flown closed loop, and the Q3 KPI matrix is not replayed (720 of 5,509 Q3 episodes replay at their revision). ARCH-31: no flight firmware.
WP3-07: G2, G4, G6, X2 and X3 flew in hs10 (14 Nov), but SET_MODE LOITER is
driven on no hil flight (the Loiter held in GUIDED, lab decision 7, PI confirmation pending); G6's
battery reason flew once on 25 Nov (bat1125_001, not KPI-bearing; ntFIN5 refused the cell). Would complete: the 19 censored runs flown long enough
and the 2 unaccepted cell-seeds flown again, WP3-15 (if 4 errors or fewer);
the report's delivery, WP4-17.

**What an external reviewer might still dispute.**

1. The pilot. Every KPI-grade run flies a stub (guardrail/vla_stub.py
   GotoPilot, ConstantPilot, StubVLA), where the grant's `hil` has the Orin
   run "VLA + Shield". The figures measure the Shield and its FSM in front
   of ArduPilot SITL under scripted commands, not a learned model (ARCH-20).
2. P0 escape is the Shield's own count, as the grant defines it ("Counter
   from Shield repair log"). On the true position ArduPilot carried the
   aircraft into a P0 zone in 21 Shield-on missions: 18 GeoFence breaches
   with RTL where a zone appeared 3 m ahead at 4 or 8 m/s, and 3 RTL legs
   through a time-windowed zone. The supplement ntFIN2s adds 3 more
   (ntFIN2s_013-015, wind-beyond-authority lane 20 at 12 m/s, the RTL
   drift hs07 first showed; demo/out/hil_ntFIN2s/sweep.json
   `_true_state`): 24 of the 586 merged. A reviewer may count a zone
   entered.
3. Fail-safe correctness has four values on one campaign: 0.998 (r2,
   adopted), 0.977 (r), 0.892 (the labels flown with), 0.871 (a). The
   adopted reading was chosen on 10 Nov after rig flights had been scored
   on another, and the gps-dropout labels were re-derived on 12 Nov after
   nt1111. Both were committed before ntFIN2 flew (ancestors of 116214f), so
   this campaign did not tune them, but neither was pre-registered. The
   Wilson 95 % lower bound is 0.9899; the grant asks for the point value.
4. Independence. No flown cell consumes its seed: a cell's three seeds are
   three flights of one deterministic scenario that differ only by the
   rig's timing (389 distinct KPI signatures over 580 episodes), while the
   rates and the Wilson bound count them as independent.
5. Coverage. 153 of the profile's 744 episodes were refused before flight
   (69 paraphrase / prefix arms, 69 stressors SITL cannot realise, 15
   others, among them the RTL that fails, G6's scenario). The 8 missions
   that never flew are wind-beyond-authority, the family in which hs07 saw
   ArduPilot's RTL carry the aircraft into nfz-square in a 12 m/s wind. A
   first attempt at the same commit, ntFIN (175 runs, stopped part-way on
   12 Nov, no sweep written), is not in the figures; its runs are kept
   (demo/out/hil_smoke/ntFIN_*).
6. Definitions. "End to end" in the tick budget starts at the Shield
   node's receipt of the pilot's message, not at a camera frame or a VLA
   decision; repair magnitude leaves the yaw rate out; mission success is
   stricter than the grant's; time to safe averages only the recovered
   spells (75 left out); theta judges about a third of the converged
   repair ticks.
7. Provenance. The 13 Nov KPI reports were generated from trees with
   uncommitted changes (`116214f3e6ee-dirty`, and `72dd5357b65b-dirty` for
   ntFIN2 with the supplement) and are outside the repository. The start-up
   tick fix (5f5009c) is on master since d7bb6a2 and has flown only in the
   shakedown hs09 (2f22116, 17 missions), not in a KPI campaign.
